MPI Corporation  | Asia Pacific
Potential further upside in
2027/28 capacity expansion;
stay OW
MPI hosted its 3Q26 earnings call on November 16. Key takeaways:
There could be further upside in 2027/28 capacity expansion: The company
reiterated that it would add at least 2.5mn pins for MEMS and 0.5mn pins for VPC in
2027. It may raise the numbers after its budget plan is finalized in December. MPI
expects to maintain aggressive capacity expansion in 2028, given strong demand.

2027 guidance appears conservative: The company guided for 2027 revenue to be
up at least 50% YoY with gross margin >60% –   conservative vs. consensus
expectations of 2027 revenue doubling. However, we expect MPI to beat its own
guidance  and raise it gradually, per previous practice of amendment.
In qualification for second insertion 2 customer: The company is  still in
qualification for insertion 2. However, it confirmed it has a second significant

customer; the schedule of the qualification could be similar to the first one. MPI said
it would be the only supplier for the first  customer's  insertion 2 process. CPO could
make up a double-digit share of 2028 total revenue.

Key Takeaways
CPO revenue should come mostly   in 2H27/2028, given the equipment lead time
and customers' verification.
It could make up a double-digit  share of 2028 total revenue.
A further 2027 capacity update is due by the end of the year.
The company guided for 4Q26 revenue to be up 5-10% QoQ, with margin similar
to 3Q26's level.

Stay OW – we cite strong AI/HPC demand,  increasing pin count  for the leading-
edge products, and the capability to pass increasing costs on to customers.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
                        MPI Corporation (6223.TWO)                    
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture 
the long-term value. We assume a 9.78% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium 
6.0%), a payout ratio of 67%, a medium-term growth rate of 16.0%, and a terminal growth rate 
of 4.0%. Our price target implies 50x/25x 2027e/2028e P/E. We believe its valuation will con-
tinue to climb along with growing earnings. 
                        Risks to Upside                    
n Stronger-than-expected AI demand
n Stronger-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Stronger-than-expected CPO development

Risks to Downside                    
n Slower-than-expected AI demand
n Slower-than-expected market share gain from peers
n Slower-than-expected CPO development

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Merida (9914.TW)
3Q Briefing Takeaway – New Model Cycle to Drive 2027 Recovery; SBC 
Visibility Remains Limited 
CITI'S TAKE 
We expect Merida’s core business to recover in 2027E, supported by 
accelerating new model launches and largely normalized EU inventories. 
However, we expect the recovery in 2027E to be gradual with US demand 
remaining sluggish. Meanwhile, despite improving pre-tax profits, SBC’s 
profitability remains highly uncertain. We trim our TP to NT$67 factoring in 
a lowered NTM P/E of 11x and earnings cuts. Remain Neutral as we see 
limited near-term catalysts and prefer to await clearer evidence of 
sustainable SBC profitability and stronger core earnings momentum.
Outlook: More new model launches in 2027E to drive a sales comeback — Mgmt. 
attributed softer shipments this year to deliberate reductions in existing models 
ahead of more new product launches in 2027E. Both Merida’s own brand and its 
major OEM customer, SBC, have adopted this strategy, aiming to preserve pricing 
discipline and avoid excessive discounting. Mgmt. expects the inventory 
adjustment to conclude by year-end and most new models are scheduled to begin 
shipping from Jan-27, paving the way for a sales recovery in 2027E.  
EU leads the recovery; China stabilizes while the US remains sluggish — Merida’s 
own brand grew 14% y/y in 9M26 in EU, supported by solid demand for road and 
gravel bikes. The mgmt. believe inventory normalization is largely complete in EU. 
China’s own brand sales grew 1% y/y in 9M26, better than the original expectation 
of DD% decline, helped primarily by entry and mid end bikes. Mgmt. expects another 
single digit sales growth in China in 2027E, supported by resilient commuter 
demand. The recovery of US market, on the other hand, is lagging EU and China amid 
inflationary pressure and persisting macro uncertainties.   
Still low visibility on SBC profitability —The weak bottom-line of SBC was primarily 
due to a conservative effective tax rate assumptions rather than a deterioration of 
its fundamentals. SBC’s 9M26 pre-tax profits grew 42% y/y or +20% y/y excluding 
US tariff refunds, reflecting improving operating performance and cost discipline. 
While improving pre-tax profitability is encouraging, we remain cautious on SBC’s 
near-term earnings contribution until there is a greater clarity on its normalized 
effective tax rate and the sustainability of US profitability. (continued)

Earnings revisions and implications — We lower our earnings estimates by 20%
for 2026E and 6% for 2027E/2028E, mainly reflecting weaker-than-expected core
sales and profitability, alongside continued uncertainty surrounding SBC’s
profitability. We cut our TP to NT$67 based on 11x 4Q26-3Q27E P/E, down from
15.5x, factoring in weaker-than-expected core business and low visibility of SBC.
While we acknowledge the potential for a stronger sales recovery in 2027E as

Merida accelerates its new model launches, we believe the recovery will be gradual
rather than a meaningful acceleration in the underlying market demand.
Meanwhile, SBC’s uncertain net profitability and lingering US market weakness
remain the key overhang.  We would prefer to stay on the sidelines, awaiting clearer
evidence of sustainable SBC net profitability and stronger earnings momentum
from the core business. Reiterate Neutral.

### Page 5

Merida (9914.TW)
17 November 2026 Citi Research
5
Merida
Company description
Merida is the second-largest bike manufacturer in Taiwan. Founded in 1972,
Merida was initially involved in OEM manufacturing before it started
marketing bikes under its own Merida brand in 1986. Merida has expanded its
footprint globally through its Merida brand and three OEM brands:
Specialized (in which Merida has a 35% stake), Centurion (51% stake), and
Miyata (45%). Merida's own brand contributes around 30-40% of total

revenues. Merida has established a proprietary network of stores throughout
China, with 16 wholly owned subsidiaries and close to 2,000 retailers that sell
only Merida-branded bikes.
Investment strategy
We rate Merida shares as Neutral. Although bike inventories have normalised
in the major markets after aggressive clearance over the past 2 years, slower-
than-expected bike demand recovery continues to weigh on Merida's topline
momentum. The EU market appears to be where we should see better growth

for 2026E, while China market also turns better than expected from 2Q26; yet
with low visibility of SBC profit trend and limited positive catalysts in sight,
we expect the share price to be range bound.
Valuation
Our target price for Merida shares of NT$93 is based on a 4Q26-3Q27E P/E of
11x, a 50% discount to its historical average of 22x during pre-Covid years
(2014-2019), in view of soft order momentum as a result of slow recovery in
bike demand, a volatile margin trend, and low visibility of SBC.

Risks
Downside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade below our target
price include: 1) e-bike adoption slower than expected; 2) weaker restocking
demand; 3) lukewarm sell-through at SBC; and 4) a weaker macro.
Upside risks that could mean the Merida shares trade above our target price
include: 1) e-bike adoption faster than expected; 2) stronger restocking
demand; 3) stronger sell-through at SBC; and 4) a strong macro.
If you are visually impaired and would like to speak to a Citi representative regarding the details

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Higher memory costs are squeezing handset makers’ budgets for
displays, touch controllers and sensors. We expect weak
Android volumes to limit DDIC suppliers’ ability to pass on
higher wafer and packaging costs, even where new OLED
programs lift content per phone.
Android bears most of the decline. Our US hardware team (2026 AlphaWise

Smartphone Survey: Another Down Year That's Not Yet Priced In) expects 2027
smartphone shipments to fall 5%, versus 4% growth previously ( Exhibit 2 ). The
team's bear case is a global decline of 11%. The base case has Android down about
9% to 768mn units and iPhone up 7% to 270mn. Across the US, China, the UK and
Germany, 41% of owners were extremely likely to upgrade within 12 months, down
2ppt ( Exhibit 3 ). The survey implies a replacement cycle of 4.4 years in 2027, up

from 4.2. We expect Android supply chains to face the greatest pressure in 2027:
falling shipments, continued de-specing, and margin compression leave Android
OEMs and suppliers more exposed as brands attempt to offset higher component
costs through a combination of price increases and bill of materials (BOM)
reductions.
China's replacement demand is weakening. We expect a 7% decline in China
smartphone shipments in 2027, following an estimated 12% decline in 2026. Among

China survey respondents, 48% were extremely likely to upgrade within 12 months,
down from 49% last year. When asked why they chose their current phone, 17%
cited built-in AI, down from 22%, while 35% cited overall device quality, up from
32% ( Exhibit 4 ).
Brand switching favors Apple in China ( Exhibit 6 ). Among respondents in China,
Apple had the strongest net switching intention at +28% of its installed base. The
corresponding rates for Xiaomi, Oppo, Vivo and Honor ranged from -21% to -31%. A

shift in purchases toward Apple would put further further pressure on component
demand at these four Android brands.
Stock implications: Egis: Rating downgraded to UW and price target lowered from
NT$105 to NT$66; we estimate wider losses in 2026, lower our 2027 EPS estimate
25% and introduce 2028 estimates. Himax: Remain EW, price target down from US
$17.4 to US$9.2, 2026/27/28 EPS estimates down 17%/17%/33%. Goodix: Remain
UW, PT down from Rmb58 to Rmb40, 2026/27/28 EPS estimates down

Himax: Earnings Revision and Quarterly Financials
We lower 2026/27/28 EPS estimates 17%/17%/33%: We incorporate 2Q-3Q26 actual
results. We see continued weakness in the consumer market (especially smartphones) due
to elevated component costs and weak demand. Increasing material and foundry prices
might also affect Himax's margins, as we believe it is unlikely to pass through to clients.
However, we expect 2027 to see more contributions from OLED DDIC, OLED T-con

shipments and WiseEye opportunities, which should help revenue and gross margin.
Continued OLED shipments for a mainstream model and inventory builds for new
premium phones lifted combined LCD and OLED handset IC sales in 3Q26. Management
expects smartphone driver sales to decline Q/Q in 4Q26. Its LCD touch-and-display drivers
serve the entry and mid-range segments, in which pressure to cut component costs is
greatest.

Himax: Valuation Methodology
We remain EW and lower our price target to US$9.2: This factors in our lower
expectations for 2026-28 earnings. Our beta assumption is raised from 1.3 to 1.5 (leading
to an increased cost of equity from 9.9% to 11.0%); the increase in beta reflects the higher
uncertainties around end market demand and component / foundry / OSAT cost pressure.
Our terminal growth rate assumption is cut from 3.4% to 2.0%; the new terminal growth

rate is now more in line with the macro economic growth rate of global developed
economies over the longer term. Other residual income assumptions are unchanged,
including risk-free rate of 2%, risk premium of 6%, intermediate growth rate of 8.0%.  We
lower our bull/bear-case values from US$26.0/8.7 to US$13.7/4.6.

Goodix: Estimate Revisions

Morgan Stanley Research 11
Goodix: Valuation Methodology
We maintain our UW rating. We lower our price target to Rmb40 from Rmb58: This is
based on a residual income model using our base-case forecasts. The lower price target
reflects our lower earnings estimate. We also lower our terminal growth rate assumption
from 4.9% to 2.5%. The new terminal growth rate is now more in line with the macro
economic growth rate of global economies over the longer term.

Other key RI assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 8.4%, an
intermediate growth rate of 16.5%. We lower our bull-case value to Rmb55 from Rmb80
and our bear-case value to Rmb21 from Rmb31.

Egis: Valuation methodology
We downgrade our rating to UW and lower our price target from NT$105 to NT$66:
This is our base-case scenario value derived from a residual income model. This reflects
the changes in our 2026-27 earnings estimates. We roll forward our valuation date from
2025 to 2026.
Changes in our RI assumptions include:
• Cost of equity is raised to 11.0% from 9.5% (beta raised from 1.25 to 1.50). The
increase in beta reflects the higher uncertainties around end market demand and

component / foundry / OSAT cost pressure
• Intermediate growth rate cut from 14.8% to 8.0%. The decrease in intermediate
growth rate reflects our more moderate view on the end market demand recovery
and new products ramp up schedule
• Terminal growth rate cut from 5.0% to 2.0%. The new terminal growth rate is now
more in line with the macro economic growth rate of global developed economies
over the longer term
Other key RI assumptions remain unchanged:

• Risk-free rate 2% and risk premium 6%
• Payout ratio of 40%
We lower our bull-case value from  NT$143 to NT$95 and bear-case value from NT$49 to
NT$30, implying 24x and 7x 2027 EPS, respectively, on our estimates.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.  
Buy (Maintain) 
목표주가(12M) 530,000원(하향) 
종가(2026.10.28) 285,500원 
상승여력 85.6%

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

Document 254: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

Document 272: AI颠覆市场调研？高露洁发表论文：让AI“角色扮演”，就能完美预测市场需求

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI颠覆市场调研？高露洁发表论文：让AI“角色扮演”，就能完美预测市场需求.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

有市场观点据此认为，传统调研的商业模式正⾯临冲击 —— 企业不必耗时数周招募受访
者，即可在极短时间内完成上千次定向访谈模拟与定价 A/B 测试。喧嚣之外，论⽂作者与⼀
线观察者却给出了更为审慎的判断。研究团队明确表⽰，合成消费者并⾮⼈类调研的全⾯
替代品；社交平台 X 上的多位评论者也提醒，该⽅法⽣成的类⼈数据分布可能暗藏统计噪
⾳。
摘要：
⾼露洁棕榄（ Colgate-Palmolive ）与 PyMC Labs 联合发布的⼀项新研究显⽰，只要改变提问⽅
式，让⼤语⾔模型 “ ⻆⾊扮演 ” 消费者， AI 预测真实购买意愿的可靠度即可达到⼈类重测信度的
90% 。
这篇挂在预印本平台 arXiv 上的论⽂近期在海外科技与商业圈引发震动。全球企业每年在消费者
调研上投⼊数⼗亿美元，有市场观点据此认为，传统调研的商业模式正⾯临冲击 —— 企业不必
耗时数周招募受访者，即可在极短时间内完成上千次定向访谈模拟与定价 A/B 测试。
喧嚣之外，论⽂作者与⼀线观察者却给出了更为审慎的判断。研究团队明确表⽰，合成消费者

并⾮⼈类调研的全⾯替代品；社交平台 X 上的多位评论者也提醒，该⽅法⽣成的类⼈数据分布可
能暗藏统计噪⾳，在⼤众⽇化之外的⾼⻛险决策中仍有待检验。
破解 AI“ 和稀泥 ” 顽疾：告别直接打分
快消品投产前，企业通常会借助代表性消费者样本测试产品概念，最核⼼的指标是以 5 点李克特
量表（ 1 分为 “ 绝对不买 ” ， 5 分为 “ 绝对会买 ” ）衡量的购买意愿（ PI ）。这⼀延续数⼗年的流程成本
⾼昂，且受访者常出现敷衍作答、顺从偏差和积极性偏差，导致需求信号充满噪⾳。
学界与业界早有引⼊ AI 充当 “ 合成消费者 ” 的尝试，但直接让模型打 1 ⾄ 5 分（直接李克特评分，
DLR ）往往收效甚微。论⽂测试 OpenAI 的 GPT-4o 与⾕歌 Gemini-2.0-flash 后发现，直接打分
时，模型习惯躲进居中的 “ 安全分 ”3 分，极少给出 1 分或 5 分，⽽真实⼈类答案⼤多集中在 4 分和 5
分。
结果，两款模型的响应分布相似度（ KS 相似度）分别低⾄ 0.26 和 0.39 。若通过修改提⽰词强⾏

把模型推向⾼分区，模型⼜会出现 “ 过度校正 ” ，导致产品概念的相对排名信号失真。
研究团队认为，问题不在于⼤语⾔模型本⾝，⽽在于直接索要数字的提问⽅式。为此，论⽂提
出语义相似度评分（ SSR ）：先向模型输⼊特定⼈⼝统计学特征和产品概念图，让其⾃由写下购
买想法；再⽤ OpenAI 的⽂本嵌⼊模型（text-embedding-3-small）提取⽂本向量，计算其
与 5 句预设锚定陈述的余弦相似度，并在 6 组参考陈述集上取均值，最终转化为 5 点量表的概率分
布。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

这套零样本（ zero-shot ）框架⽆需历史调研数据训练或微调，具备即插即⽤的低成本优势，其
Python 代码已在 GitHub 开源。
9300 份真实样本实测：跑赢传统算法，但离不开 “ ⼈设 ”
研究团队调取了某领先个护企业在美国市场完成的 57 项真实概念调研作为基准。每项调研包含
150 ⾄ 400 名独⽴受访者，总样本量达 9300 ⼈。
在完整画像与图⽂概念刺激下， SSR 将 GPT-4o 和 Gemini-2.0-flash 在产品相对排名上的相关性
达成率分别推升⾄ 90.2% 和 90.6% （以模拟 2000 次⼈类随机分组重测的相关性上限为参照）。两
款模型的分布 KS 相似度同步跃升⾄ 0.88 和 0.80 ，⼤幅甩开直接打分法，也⾼于先写⽂本再交由
AI“ 评分专家 ” 转译的追问打分法（ FLR ，两款模型分别为 0.72 和 0.59 ）。

### Page 3

在泛化与降维测试中， SSR 同样表现稳健。⾯对 “ 产品概念与你的相关性如何 ” 这⼀新问题，
Gemini-2.0-flash 的相关性达成率达 82% ，分布相似度达 0.81 ；若将图⽂概念简化为纯⽂本，模
型表现仅轻微下滑。
更关键的对照来⾃传统机器学习。研究⼈员随机抽取 28 项调研作为训练集、 29 项作为测试集，
⽤ 5 项⼈⼝特征和 3 项产品属性重复训练了 300 个梯度提升决策树模型（ LightGBM ）。即便掌握样
本内数据， LightGBM 的相关性达成率也仅为 64.6% ，分布相似度为 0.80 ；同等划分下，零样本
GPT-4o 配合 SSR 分别拿到 88.3% 和 0.88 。
但实验同时划出⼀条红线： “ ⼈设 ” 不可或缺。⼀旦从 Gemini-2.0-flash 的提⽰词中剔除所有⼈⼝
统计学特征，合成样本虽能产出与真实数据⼀致的平均分（ 4.0±0.1 ）和⾼达 0.91 的分布相似
度，但在最佳参考集下，产品排名的相关性达成率从 92% 断崖式跌⾄ 50% 。缺少画像约束的 AI

只会⼀味给好评，失去筛选产品的核⼼功能。
细分维度上，合成消费者较好还原了真实消费规律。真实调研中购买意愿随年龄呈倒 U 型分布
（中年⾼于年轻与⽼年）， GPT-4o 准确复现了这⼀⾛势；⾯对预算紧张的收⼊设定、不同品类
与价格档位， AI 的偏好排序也与⼈类⾼度贴合。
附带的⾃由⽂本还带来了意外红利。真实受访者的开放式留⾔往往⽌步于 “ 挺好的 ” ， AI 却能给出
详尽且少有积极性偏差的批评，其预测均值跨度更⼴，反⽽为早期筛选提供了更具区分度的信
号。⾯对不受欢迎的概念， Gemini-2.0-flash 会直⾔： “ 听起来很贵，我不太买账那些 ‘ 微⽣物组 ’
的说法，还是⽤我熟悉的产品吧。 ”
颠覆⾏业还是 “ 随机鹦鹉 ” ？
论⽂作者在结论中勾勒了新的研发路径：企业⽆需为每个早期创意买单，⽽是先⽤合成消费者
低成本海选，将昂贵的⼈类样本留给最具潜⼒的候选⽅案。这既能加速迭代，也让中⼩品牌有

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
机会触及以往⽆⼒承担的消费者洞察。
不过，研究团队并未回避⽅法的硬伤。实验中采⽤的 6 组锚定陈述系针对这 57 项调研⼿动优化，
跨调研的普适性仍待观察； AI 虽能较好模拟年龄与收⼊差异，在性别、居住地区和族裔维度上却
表现不稳。
更根本的制约来⾃训练语料。 SSR 在⼝腔与个护领域奏效，很⼤程度上源于⽹络论坛和消费者
评论中沉淀了海量相关讨论；⼀旦进⼊语料稀缺的陌⽣品类，⼤模型仍会产⽣幻觉。真实世界
⾥的预算硬约束、⽂化语境与营销曝光，同样难以被合成样本完整捕捉。
社交平台 X 上的争论折射出业界对这⼀趋势的分歧。⽤⼾ James He 将语义相似度诱导称作 “ ⼀个
有趣的⼩技巧 ” ，指出其制造类⼈分布的⽅式 “ 本质上只是噪⾳，且丢失了项⽬间的所有协⽅
差 ” ， “ ⽤来决定⽛膏⽅案或许⾜够，但未必适⽤于最关键的决策 ” 。

⽤⼾ James Sutton 则认为这正是 “ 随机鹦鹉 ” 的典型写照：⼤模型输出的只是 “ 互联⽹平均值的均
值 ” ，⻆⾊扮演本质上是基于条件逻辑的简化世界构建，虽⾮真实思考，却依然管⽤。
也有观察者看到了这项成果的潜⼒。⽤⼾ MartianCats 评论称，商业⼴告⽆处不在地运⽤ “ 选择
架构 ” 引导消费，如今⼤模型开始直接对⼈类⾏为建模， “ 尚不清楚⾏为经济学家如何看待⼤模
型，但它们或许会像近期改变数学领域⼀样，颠覆经济学 ” 。

Document 273: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

Document 336: FICC日报：宏观情绪再次下探，关注APEC会议

### Page 1

FICC日报 | 2026-11-17

宏观情绪再次下探，关注APEC会议 
市场分析 
关注美联储加息计价上升可能。美国10月非农数据大超预期，新增16.5万人，且薪资增速超预期，美国10月核心
CPI环比上涨0.3%，高于市场预期的0.2%，整体CPI环比上涨0.4%，同样超出预期的0.3%。经济韧性的背景叠加油
价短期居高不下，通胀向其他领域传导或将更顺畅，中期选举后多为美联储票委发表鹰派讲话，需要警惕加息计
价上升的可能。此外，截至11月9日，10Y法德利差录得1.476%，较峰值下降13BP，高于10Y意德利差的1.04%，短
期宏观风险略有缓和。 
中东局势再度反复。随着共和党在中期选举上失利，美国进一步推进美伊和谈的意愿上升，但若伊朗方要价过高
和谈也较难推进，此前美伊双方谈判重点有所分歧，美方聚焦伊朗去核化，伊朗聚焦美国撤军。11月8日美国总统
特朗普在接受媒体采访时明确表示不打算升级与伊朗的战事，同时美方媒体还透露万斯过去两周积极推动新停火
并试图恢复核谈。11月11日，伊朗最高国家安全委员会秘书雷扎伊表示，伊朗不会让一点浓缩铀运出境。11月12
日，特朗普表示“不需要与伊朗达成协议 达成协议的时代早已过去”。随着特朗普的最新表态，美伊局势朝着长期
僵持局势发展的概率正持续上升。 
国内稳信心政策陆续出台。按美元计，中国10月出口同比增长41.6%，进口同比增35.2%，均高于预期。10月投资、
消费数据表现偏弱。10月20日发改委新闻发布会释放正在研究制定扩大内需战略实施方案，11月2日央行推出超
1000亿证券、基金、保险公司互换便利(SFISF)推动市场信心企稳。关注11月17-18日的APEC会议。 
分板块来看，关注农产品的机会。民主党全面掌控国会压制铜关税预期以及油价，短期利空铜价、利好黄金，远
端则需要关注加息预期的变化。能源方面，尽管美伊局势仍往僵持方向发展，但实际中东的原油出口量明显回升，
在不造成生产设施破坏的前提下，油品及化工将温和调整。农产品则关注厄尔尼诺的气候预期，以及对应减产影
响，10月份USDA报告出现产量不及预期的信号，农产品有望重新启动。

策略 
商品和股指期货：部分农产品逢低做多。

风险 
地缘政治风险（能源板块上行风险）；全球经济超预期下行（风险资产下行风险）；美联储超预期收紧（风险资产
下行风险）；海外流动性风险冲击（风险资产下行风险）。

Document 352: GPT-6.2自己造出全新证法，94分钟拿下20年前顶刊定理首证

Source PDF: all report pdf/GPT-6.2自己造出全新证法，94分钟拿下20年前顶刊定理首证.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

Vals AI 最新⾼难数学基准测试中， GPT-6.2 Astra 以 87.50% 的准确率登顶。⾯对 Lean 公
共数学库缺失的三个关键定理， Astra ⾃主定义 “ 容量 ” 这⼀新数学⼯具，绕开原论⽂证明路
线，仅⽤ 94 分钟、 1794 ⾏ Lean 代码，完成 Smoktunowicz 间隙定理的全球⾸个形式化证
明。
摘要：
刚刚，第三⽅评测机构 Vals AI 放出了⾼难数学基准 Autoform-Hard 的最新榜单。
56 道博⼠与科研级数学难题，此前 10 个前沿⼤模型去测，结果⼀道都没做出来。
⽽在这轮测试⾥， Claude Fable 5.2 和 GPT-6.2 Sol 双双做对 48 道，打成平⼿。
唯独 GPT-6.2 Astra 多做对了第 49 道题，拿下了榜单第⼀。
这道唯⼀拉开差距的第 49 题，正是 2006 年发在数学四⼤顶刊上的Smoktunowicz 间隙定理。
在全程零⼈⼯⼲预的环境⾥， Astra 只⽤了94 分钟、写下 1,794 ⾏ Lean 代码，就拿下了它的全球

⾸个形式化证明。
⽐「全球⾸证」更让⼈意外的，是它解出这道题的⽅式。
原作者 2006 年的论⽂，后半程要⽤到三个现成的数学⼤定理，可 Astra 能调⽤的 Lean 公共数学库
⾥⼀个都没有。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

换作以前的 AI ，只要数学库⾥缺了关键的前置结论，推导当场就会卡在半路。
但 Astra 在原路⾛不通之后，居然在半路⾃⼰定义了⼀个叫「 capacity 」（容量）的新数学⼯具，
绕开那三个⼤定理，⾛出了⼀条全新的证明路线。
复核完所有代码后， Vals AI 团队在报告⾥写道，这是对 Smoktunowicz 定理本⾝的形式化证
明，⽽⾮对原作者证明过程的翻译。
2 和 3 之间有块禁区，原证明的三个定理都不在库⾥
这条 Smoktunowicz 间隙定理，研究的是代数的「增⻓速度」。
从⼏个基本元素出发，把它们最多相乘 n 次，数⼀数⼀共能造出多少个彼此独⽴的元素。
数学家⽤⼀个叫GK 维数（ Gelfand-Kirillov 维数）的指数，来衡量这个数量涨得有多快。
如果数量⼤致按 n² 增⻓， GK 维数就是 2 ，按 n³ 增⻓， GK 维数就是 3 。
对⼀般的代数来说，这个指数只要在 2 以上，本来可以取任意实数。但⼀旦加上特定的结构限
制，数轴上就会出现⼀段不可能落⼊的「禁区」。

1995 年，代数学家 Artin 和 Stafford 研究了⼀类叫分次整环的代数，它的元素可以按次数⼀层层分
开，⽽且两个⾮零元素相乘不会等于 0 。
当时他们证明，这类代数的 GK 维数不可能落在 2 到 2.2 之间。
到了 2006 年，爱丁堡⼤学的波兰数学家 Agata Smoktunowicz 把这个范围⼀举推到了 3 。
她证明，满⾜条件的分次整环，GK 维数要么不超过 2 ，要么⾄少是 3 ， 2 和 3 之间的所有实数统统
不可能出现。

### Page 3

这 篇 解 决 了 代 数 学 界 ⼗ 余 年 难 题 的 论 ⽂ ， 发 表 在 数 学 四 ⼤ 顶 刊 之 ⼀ 的 《 I n v e n t i o n e s
Mathematicae 》上，全⽂却只有短短 6 ⻚。
篇幅之所以这么短，是因为作者在后半程连续调⽤了前⼈已经证好的三个⼤定理。
它们分别是Bergman 间隙定理、Small-Warfield 定理，以及代数曲线上的黎曼 - 罗赫定理。
⼈类写论⽂可以直接引⽤前⼈的现成结论，但在 Lean ⾥，只要引⽤的定理没在代码库⾥证过，
代码就⽆法通过编译。
最棘⼿的是，原论⽂⽤到的这三个⼤定理， Lean 的公共数学库 Mathlib ⾥⼀个都没有收录。
⽽ Autoform-Hard 给每道题的限时只有 3 ⼩时，如果先把这三个⼤定理从头写成代码，本⾝就是
⼀项⼤⼯程，规定时间内根本做不完。
花 8 美元想放弃被打回，停笔 9 分钟重找路线
Vals AI 公开的 146 轮完整运⾏⽇志，还原了 Astra 在考场上的真实破局过程。

开局后的前 17 分钟，它⼀直在后台推演思路，光思考就⽤掉了约 3.4 万个推理 Token 。

### Page 4

第 18 分钟起，它才开始照着原论⽂的反证法框架写前半程代码，也就是先假设 GK 维数落在 2 和 3
之间，此时每⼀层的维数「⽐线性增⻓快、⼜⽐平⽅增⻓慢」。
接着它利⽤Ore 条件把代数放进可做除法的分式除环⾥，只要能反过来推导出「每⼀层的维数最
多只能线性增⻓」，就能制造⽭盾排除掉 2 到 3 。
可前半程刚搭完，⻢上就遇到了那三个 Mathlib ⾥没有的⼤定理，推导到了第 47 分钟便卡住了。
当时 Astra 试着交上⼀份还没填完的稿⼦，因为关键步骤还留着表⽰跳过的 sorry 标记，被判分器
当场拒收。
1 分钟后， Astra ⼲脆提交了⼀份「未解决」报告，如实列出了已经写完的模块和卡住的缺⼝，准
备放弃这道题。
偏偏在这时， Autoform-Hard 的⼀条规则拦住了它。
测试系统规定，模型必须花满 20 美元的算⼒额度才允许放弃，不到额度交上来的放弃报告⼀律
驳回。
⽽ Astra 当时只花了⼤约8 美元，系统当场退回报告，要求它留下来继续解题。
原路已经⾛不通，交卷放弃⼜被系统拦了回来，它只能重新寻找出路。

从第 49 到第 56 分钟， Astra 整整 9 分钟没有写⼀⾏代码。
这段时间⾥，它⼀边检索数学库⾥现有的可⽤结论，⼀边花了约 1.9 万个推理 Token 重新推演路
线，其中最⻓的⼀次连续思考持续了整整 4 分钟。
重新动笔后，它先证出分式除环⾥「次数为 0 」的那部分（记作 D ）可以由⼀⼩块有限维空间⽣
成，把剩下的难题收束到如何直接控制 D 的增⻓。
⼀条命令写出「容量」新⼯具， 11 分半钟⾛通新证明

### Page 5

真正的转折点出现在第 75 分钟。
在连续 5 轮查库和思考后，它的推理摘要⾥出现了⼀句「我得把容量的代码抽象出来」。
紧接着，它⽤⼀条命令⼀次性写出了核⼼⽂件 Capacity.lean ，全篇 133 ⾏代码、 25 个数学声
明，仅改了⼀轮⼩错， 22 秒后就编译通过。
在这个⽂件⾥， Astra ⾃⼰定义了⼀个叫「容量」（ capacity ）的新指标，⽤来计算 D ⾥的⼀块空
间做⼀次乘法后，维数⾄少会增加多少。
它很快证出了这个指标的⼀条核⼼性质 ——
两块空间合并后的容量不会超过各⾃容量之和（次模性），把空间做平移也不会改变数值。
由此就能推出整套⽅案最关键的结论，所有「不会让容量增加」的元素放在⼀起，刚好构成⼀
个可以做加减乘除的稳定⼦除环。
有了这个稳定⼦除环，想要控制整个 D ，就只需要控制住⽣成 D 的那⼀⼩块空间。
随后， Astra 把容量放到⼀层层越来越⼤的空间上逐步计算。
因为它是整数，⽽代数的增⻓⼜⽐平⽅慢，这个数值到了某⼀步之后必然会进⼊平台期。
在平台期⾥，那个稳定⼦除环已经把整个 D 都包了进来。

这样⼀来，代数每往上⼀层，维数最多只多出⼀个固定上限的常数，每⼀层的维数增⻓被限制
在线性以内，与开头「⽐线性快」的假设⽭盾，反证完成。
原论⽂依赖的那三个⼤定理，它从头到尾既没有调⽤，也没有去补证，靠⾃⼰定义的新⼯具绕
了过去。

### Page 6

从写出 Capacity.lean 到第 87 分钟⽤ 7 ⾏顶层代码通过判分器核验， Astra 全程只⽤了11 分半钟，
连续写完了后半程的 6 个模块，并在清理完试错代码后正式交卷。
唯⼀拉开差距的第 49 题， AI 开始⾛出⾃⼰的证法

### Page 7

回看整份 Autoform-Hard 榜单，这 56 道赛题全部选⾃ Meta FAIR 今年发布的 AutoformBench 基
准，取材于 24 本研究⽣与科研级数学教材。
GPT-6.2 Astra 最终以 87.50% 的准确率登顶，攻克这道间隙定理全程只花了 21.58 美元，领先并
列 85.71% 的 Claude Fable 5.2 与 GPT-6.2 Sol 。
AutoformBench 的作者之⼀ Charles Arnal 也在 X 上转发感叹，⼤规模数学形式化变成廉价⽇常⼯
具的那⼀天已经越来越近了。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
赛后， Vals AI 团队还专⻔追溯了「容量」这套证法的来源。
为了确认 Astra 是不是背过了现成答案，研究⼈员把 1995 年和 2006 年的两篇原论⽂逐⻚核对了⼀
遍，确认⾥⾯根本没有对应的论证。
进⼀步检索⽂献后，他们发现， Astra 是把 1998 年另⼀篇代数论⽂⾥的维数亏损量，和组合数学
⾥的等周极⼩化技巧结合在了⼀起，在测试中⾃⼰拼出了⼀条新路线。
甚⾄连 Vals AI 团队查完⽂献后也不确定，此前有没有⼈把这两套⼯具这样搭配⽤过，特意在报
告末尾留下邮箱公开征集线索。
从照着⼈类论⽂逐⾏翻译，到⾛出⼀套连评测机构都要向学界求证出处的新证法， AI 做数学已
经迈出了新的⼀步。
本⽂来源：新智元

### Page 9

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, declined to –0.14% in October
from +0.06% in September.

The Leading Index weakened again in October after steadier
results through August-September. At –0.14% the latest six
month growth rate is back in negative, below-trend territory,
broadly similar to the soft reads around the start of the year.
The shift points to a return to below-trend growth momentum
in the first half of 2027. Westpac expects annual GDP growth
to slow back to 1.4% for calendar 2026, with a further
moderation to a sluggish 1%yr pace in 2027.

The Leading Index growth rate is still a touch firmer than
six months ago, the current pace of –0.14% comparing to a
–0.2% back in April, when the War in Iran was first impacting.
The component detail shows mixed moves over the
intervening period. Four components have started to exert
a bigger drag: total hours worked taking –0.2ppts off the
headline growth rate; with a further 0.15ppt drag coming
from the combined effect of softer commodity prices

(measured in AUD terms); a narrowing yield spread (mainly
reflecting the RBA tightening pushing up short term rates);
and rising job loss fears captured by the Westpac-Melbourne
Institute Unemployment Expectations Index. This has been
more than offset by a reduced (but still negative) drag from
the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Expectations
Index (adding +0.25ppts); and firmer reads on local equity
markets and dwelling approvals (adding +0.17ppts on a

combined basis.
Overall, the component mix shows labour market conditions
and tightening monetary policy have become more prominent
drags on momentum. While the Leading Index signal is not
overly weak the decline is clearly worth monitoring with the
detail suggesting there may be more weakening to come.
Leading Index moves back below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines

variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken
individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

Document 384: New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/New Zealand 2026 General Election_ Preliminary results and next steps (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• As polls suggested, the election was a nailbiter
that has not yet been resolved. Election night
results show a lead to the centre-right on
paper in a significantly enlarged parliament.
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First
and ACT won 67 seats. The centre-left bloc
of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti Māori won 57
seats. There was a swing away from National
to its coalition partners while the centre left
grouping picked up a few seats.

• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling
and will enter parliament with at least 7 seats,
reducing what could have been a larger swing
to the centre-left grouping.
• The size of parliament will expand by around 9
seats as National held onto a large share of its
electorate votes, entitling it to more seats than
implied by its party vote share. As a result,
a bloc would require 66 seats to command a
majority in parliament.
• Several senior National ministers have not

been re-elected, including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. She will continue in a caretaker
role but will need to be replaced in any new
centre-right government.
• Significant uncertainty about the result exists.
National might lose perhaps two marginal
electorates in the final analysis. There are
400,000 special votes to count which may
shift the list seat allocations as these tend
to reduce the share of the centre-right bloc
by 1.6-1.7ppts. Hence, when all is said and

done, the centre-right’s 10 seat advantage on
election night could reduce by 1-4 seats.
• Should this play out the centre-right would not
have sufficient seats to reach a majority in an
enlarged house and would require the support
of the Opportunity Party to govern.
• The final election outcome will not be
known until 27 November. Serious coalition
negotiations likely won’t begin until then
and delays could occur. Should recounts be
required, some further delay is possible.

• Based on these results, we see a higher
probability of a centre-right government
ultimately emerging that likely will require
the support of the Opportunity Party in some
form. The Opportunity Party likely won’t enter
Cabinet but may offer support on confidence
and supply in exchange for policy concessions.
• But a centre-left government can’t be ruled
out if special votes heavily favour the centre
left bloc, and especially if an agreement
between the Opportunity Party and the centre-

right parties can’t be reached.
• Regardless of the final count, the results
suggest a government with weak support and
ongoing vulnerabilities. Hence the prospect of
controversial large changes may be modest.
For a centre-right government that might
limit the extent of fiscal consolidation, while
the centre left’s plans for larger scale tax and
spending initiatives could be trimmed back.
• A centre-right government won’t change the
RBNZ mandate while a centre-left government

likely still will. A new finance minister will be
required with the possibility of ACT leader
David Seymour taking on that role.
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
New Zealand 2026 General Election:
Preliminary results and next steps.
9 Nov 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz
Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
The result.
The preliminary results of the 2026 General Election,
based on the election night count, are set out in the table
above. In summary:
• The centre-right bloc of National, NZ First and ACT
won 67 seats.
• The centre-left bloc of Labour, Greens and Te Pāti
Māori won 57 seats.
• The Opportunity Party delivered on its polling and will
enter parliament with 7 seats.
These results indicate a swing away from National to its

coalition partners while the centre-left grouping picked
up a few seats. The big gainer was the Opportunity Party
who perhaps took a big share of what otherwise could
have been a larger swing to the centre-left grouping.
The size of parliament will expand by around nine seats,
as National held onto a large share of its electorate
votes and hence has more seats than it would have been
entitled to based on its party vote share. As a result, a
governing bloc would require 66 seats to command a

majority in parliament.
Several of the electorate seats are very tight (see Table
2), so election night majorities could easily be overturned
once special votes are counted. Of particular relevance
is the seat of Wairarapa, where the National candidate is
leading the Labour candidate by just 139 votes. If Labour
were to win that seat (specials favoured Labour in 2020
but National in 2023), all else equal the centre-right seat
count would decline to 66 seats in what would become

a 130-seat Parliament – just enough to command a
majority. But if either NZ First or ACT were to lose a single
party vote seat to the left bloc once specials are counted
then that majority would disappear.
On that score, historically the election night results have
tended to overstate the support of the centre-right bloc
and understate the support for parties on the left of
the political spectrum. For example, over the previous
three elections (2017, 2020, 2023), the current centre-

right bloc’s vote share has declined 1.9%, 1.7% and 1.6%
respectively following the counting of special votes, with
the National Party losing 2 seats on each occasion once
the official results were declared (Labour, the Greens and
Te Pāti Māori have benefited). On this occasion, given its
overhang, a lower party vote share would not lower the
National Party’s final vote count, although it could alter
the size of its overhang and thus the number of seats

required to command a majority. In recent elections both
NZ First and ACT have seen their vote shares decline
following the counting of special votes.
The process from here.
Official results.
The official results will be declared on 27 November,
adding the results of an estimated 400,000 special votes
(with around a quarter of these cast overseas) to the
2.41m votes counted on election night.
We expect discussions between potential coalition
partners will begin immediately to start sketching out the

parameters of a coalition agreement. But given the tight
margins of the current vote count, no firm positions will
be agreed until the results are declared.
The writ and recounts.
After 27 November, the writ is scheduled to be returned
on 3 December – formally certifying who has been
elected. However, that date is subject to any recounts

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
that might be requested. Parliament is required to meet
no later than 14 January (six weeks after the return of the
writ), providing an opportunity for a new government
to test that it has the confidence of the House. It is
worth noting that Parliament can be summoned and
begin functioning even if coalition negotiations are still
occurring. If no coalition or confidence-and-supply
arrangement has been reached by then, negotiations can

simply continue and the incumbent Government remains
in office under the caretaker convention (Ministers in
the outgoing government retain executive authority
temporarily, even if they are no longer MPs).
Given that the Pre-Election Economic and Fiscal
Update was released in late September there is no legal
requirement for the Treasury to publish a Half Year
Economic and Fiscal Update (HYEFU) this year. And given
that it will be some weeks before a government is formed,

it seems unlikely that there will be a government in place
that would request that a HYEFU be produced despite
there being no statutory requirement (certainly there
would be little time to incorporate policy changes that
fall out of new coalition agreements, thus diminishing
its value). Once a government is formed, it will have an
opportunity to lay out its immediate priorities in the
Budget Policy Statement (BPS), which by law needs to
be released before the end of March next year. The new

government could decide to release an interim fiscal
update with the BPS if that was thought necessary.
Potential coalition outcomes.
We see three main likely outcomes:
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First.
• A three-party majority coalition between National, ACT
and NZ First with the support of the Opportunity Party
on confidence and supply.
• Some coalition of Labour, the Greens, Opportunity and
Te Pāti Māori, potentially with Opportunity and Te Pāti

Māori sitting outside the cabinet.
Our preview of the election discussed the policies of
the main political parties, while the figure on page 5
highlights some points of commonality with the centre-
right and centre-left blocs.
National, ACT and NZ First share a broadly pro-
development and deregulatory agenda, including support
for greater resource development, fewer regulatory
barriers and compulsory or expanded KiwiSaver. Beyond
that common core, National and ACT align particularly

closely on lower taxes and deregulation, while National
and NZ First share an emphasis on infrastructure, regional
development and law and order. ACT and NZ First favour
tighter immigration settings and tighter welfare policies.
The differences are clearest at the edges: ACT advocates
deeper tax cuts, smaller government and more market-
led provision, while NZ First places greater emphasis
on economic sovereignty, regional development and
targeted support for working New Zealanders. National

occupies more of the middle ground, combining fiscal
discipline and a pro-business agenda with increased
infrastructure and housing supply.
The Opportunity Party presents itself as a middle
alternative that can work with the left or the right
groupings. They have five bottom line policies they say
they need to support any new government. These include
competition reform through enhanced Commerce
Commission powers, more restrictions on political
lobbyists and donors, a ten-year plan of investment in the

healthcare sector, an end to bottom trawling in inshore
areas and a phase out elsewhere and for the Government
to recommit to New Zealand’s Paris Agreement
obligations. Their success in meeting these bottom
lines with a new government and their flexibility around
these requirements will be key to their ability to form an
agreement with either side. There is better congruence
of the Opportunity Party’s policies with the left than the
right – so progress here will be critical to watch should

their support be required to form a new government. We
suspect they will be very motivated to avoid the need for
a new election via a hung parliament should push come
to shove.

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Labour, the Greens and Opportunity share support
for increased public and social investment, a broader
tax base, greater infrastructure investment, stronger
competition policy, cheaper public transport and a
more active role for government. Labour and the Greens
overlap particularly on higher taxation, public investment
and stronger climate and environmental policy, while
Labour and Opportunity share an emphasis on social

investment and higher public-service wages. The Greens
and Opportunity both favour broader taxation and
stronger climate action, although their preferred tax
instruments differ. Their distinctive priorities are also
clear: Labour places more weight on worker protections
and restoring the RBNZ’s dual mandate; the Greens on
environmental protection, progressive taxation and Te
Tiriti; and Opportunity on land-value taxation, housing
infrastructure, productivity, innovation and evidence-

based policy.

SOVEREIGN DEBT: WHEN SHORT-TERM SHOCKS AND SHIFTS IN DEBT STRUCTURE LEAVE A LASTING FISCAL
BURDEN — THE CASE OF BRAZIL
Salim Hammad
Brazil’s recent post-election yield shock risks leaving a lasting imprint on public finances — compounded by already high interest rates and a
changing debt structure. Large refinancing needs and a rising share of floating-rate debt will transmit rapidly and significantly higher interest

rates to the debt service through distinct but reinforcing channels.

An abrupt post-election market awakening

In the wake of the 2 nd round of the general elections in late October, Brazilian markets — which had expected a Flavio Bolsonaro
victory — sold off sharply after Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva won a fourth presidential term. Both the equity and FX markets
experienced large intraday drops (14% of the Ibovespa stock index and -11% BRL vs. USD). Meanwhile, the yield on Brazil’s 10-year
government bond rose 250 basis points to 15.5%, reaching its highest level in over a decade (Chart 1) as investors worried about

the fiscal impact of costly campaign promises amidst large and persistent fiscal imbalances.
Fiscal risks have reshaped Brazil’s public debt structure, magnifying the cost of persistently high interest rates
Absent a confidence-infusing fiscal adjustment, Brazil’s interest bill is unlikely to decline in the short term, maintaining upward
pressure on public debt (83% of GDP in October 2026) and limiting budgetary leeway.
Three transmission channels, amplified by changing features of the debt structure, explain this persistence.

The first channel: The rise in long-term yields will increase rollover costs.
The sovereign must refinance roughly 20% of its debt stock each year. An average maturity of 4.1 years spreads refinancing over
time but does not shield the interest bill from a sustained rise in borrowing costs. As maturing securities are replaced, higher
issuance rates progressively lift the average borrowing cost — even for fixed-rate debt, whose interest costs remain unchanged

until refinancing. A sustained yield shock therefore feeds into debt servicing as the stock rolls over.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 10 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The second channel: Demand for floating-rate instruments will strengthen until confidence in fiscal policy improves.
In turn, a larger share of SELIC-linked debt heightens the debt stock’s sensitivity to the prevailing high policy rate. Already SELIC-linked
securities reached a record 52.6% of the debt stock in August 2026, around 12 percentage points above their share at the start of President

Lula’s last term, versus a low of 18.4% in September 2014 (Chart 2). Weaker demand for fixed-rate securities with maturities exceeding five
years, amid increased fiscal uncertainty, has encouraged this shift 1.
The third channel: The BCB may delay rate cuts until fiscal policy over the next mandate is better defined.
A slower easing cycle would prolong high interest costs on SELIC-linked debt, whose servicing costs adjust with the policy rate without

waiting for refinancing. With these securities accounting for a growing share of the debt stock, delayed monetary easing would postpone
relief for the Treasury’s interest bill — even if long-term market yields subsequently ease.
These channels operate at different speeds: a rise in long-term yields increases rollover costs, whereas floating-rate debt reprices with
the SELIC rate, not automatically with 10-year yields. Meanwhile, a slower monetary easing cycle and stronger demand for floating-rate

securities compound pressure on the interest bill.
The fiscal–monetary policy nexus is therefore likely to remain a source of strain on Brazil’s public finances: fiscal uncertainty could delay
monetary easing, while greater floating-rate exposure would prolong the burden of high policy rates on the interest bill.
salim.hammad@bnpparibas.com
1 Fiscal slippage and uncertainty make investors less willing to lock in nominal returns over long horizons: if inflation or market yields rise, the purchasing power of fixed payments

declines or the bonds’ market value falls. Investors therefore demand higher yields to bear this risk, making long-term fixed-rate funding more expensive for the Treasury. By issuing
floating-rate bonds instead, the Treasury offers returns that adjust with the SELIC rate, sustaining demand, securing financing and reducing the risk of failed auctions. However
longer floating-rate maturities may reduce rollover risk, but do not remove the repricing exposure. Floating-rate issuance helps secure financing but transfers interest-rate risk to

the Treasury.

Document 411: The Global Economy’s Resilience to Shocks_ Four Observations and Three Intertwined Questions (2026-11-16)

Report source: BNP Paribas

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.42 / 16 November 2026EDITORIAL
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY’S RESILIENCE TO SHOCKS: FOUR OBSERVATIONS AND THREE INTERTWINED QUESTIONS
A review of the latest economic indicators reveals four key observations. First, the global economy continues to show re-
silience in the face of the energy shock, which has recently been compounded by the impact of rising long-term interest
rates. Second, this resilience still relies heavily on the global investment cycle, but not exclusively: household consump-

tion also provides support. Albeit on a more or less strong footing depending on the country, its contribution to growth
is non-negligible. Third, the rise in inflation remains primarily energy-driven, with the United States standing out unfa-
vorably from the Eurozone due to a more entrenched and persistent problem. Fourth, the rise in long-term government
yields is placing a significant, well-identified, yet manageable strain on public finances. Three intertwined questions arise

regarding the rise in long-term interest rates: How high can they go? Will growth continue to hold up against all odds?
How will central banks react?
FIRST OBSERVATION: THE GLOBAL ECONOMY CONTINUES TO WEATHER THE
ENERGY SHOCK WITHOUT BREAKING
The surge in energy prices following the outbreak of the Middle East
conflict in late February has had a smaller impact on economic acti -
vity than the 2022 energy shock caused by the invasion of Ukraine 1.

Although the price of a barrel of Brent crude once again (and largely so)
crossed the USD 100 threshold in October while European natural gas
prices (TTF) remain under pressure as winter approaches, the macroe-
conomic shockwave has remained much more contained than four
years ago. According to business climate surveys in the Eurozone and
the United States, the manufacturing sector has weathered the shock
remarkably well, while the impact was more visible in the services

sector and on consumer confidence before an improvement set in.
In October, the overall picture remains positive: while there are signs of
erosion, they remain contained. The PMI/ISM indices remain above the
50-point expansion threshold — clearly so in the US, and to a slightly
lesser extent in Europe. Emerging economies have also shown resi -
lience, thereby fueling global growth. Non-resident portfolio invest -
ment flows have certainly been highly volatile since the outbreak of the

conflict in Iran, but financing conditions have held up despite pressures
on energy prices and interest rates in advanced economies, and the
aggregate manufacturing PMI index for the fifteen major emerging eco-
nomies has nearly returned to its February 2026 level since September.
SECOND OBSERVATION: THE SUPPORTIVE ROLE OF HOUSEHOLD CONSUMP-
TION, IN THE SHADOW OF THE INVESTMENT CYCLE
In the current business cycle, there is much more talk of corporate in-

vestment than of household consumption. The former is indeed riding
high, driven by several mutually reinforcing trends: the AI boom, in -
creased military spending, decarbonization and electrification efforts,
and Germany’s massive infrastructure plan. As a result, this invest -
ment engine appears immune — or at least less sensitive, at the aggre-
gate level — to the energy shock than household consumption. Inco -
me and financing constraints appear to be more severe for household

consumption. This is evident in household confidence, which certainly
does not reflect the same level of optimism as business climate indica-
tors. And yet, while they show little confidence, they are quite spend -
thrift, particularly in the United States. Across the Atlantic, the current
investment cycle is certainly a major driver of growth, closely trailing
that of household consumption. However, until the second quarter of
2026, household consumption retained its role as the primary driver, as

having the largest contribution to growth (see chart). And while it was
dethroned in the third quarter according to currently available data, its
support remains very much present and substantial.
Household consumption in the Eurozone certainly does not show the
same momentum: on a quarterly average, from the first quarter of
2023 through the second quarter of 2026, it grew nearly half as fast
as in the United States (0.34% vs. 0.66%). Nevertheless, it remains the

dominant driver of growth in the Eurozone (with a contribution amoun-
ting to 0.2pp, or 71%, compared with half a percentage point for total
investment), without the same wealth effect as in the United States
and without a decline in the savings rate, which leaves it with greater
potential for improvement.
THIRD OBSERVATION: THE RISE IN INFLATION REMAINS MOSTLY ENER-
GY-DRIVEN BUT APPEARS MORE ENTRENCHED ACROSS THE ATLANTIC
On the inflation front, the rise continues to be primarily energy-driven,

even though it appears more entrenched in the United States, and ini-
tial signs of pass-through — notably via producer prices — will be
closely monitored, while overall upward pressures remain strong 2.
In the Eurozone, where harmonised inflation reached 4.0% year-on-
year in October 2026, energy accounts for nearly half of the rise in
prices. Core inflation remains contained (at 2.6% y/y), as do all alter -
native indicators, including inflation expectations. The situation in the

United States, however, calls for increased vigilance. While headline
inflation (3.7% y/y in September according to the CPI) is lower than the

Eurozone October print, the underlying problem is more profound: core
inflation is picking up again, and nearly 60% of the components of the
US consumer price basket are rising by more than 3% (compared with
43% in the Eurozone).
FOURTH OBSERVATION: THE RISE IN LONG-TERM RATES ADDS A NEW, SIGNI-
FICANT BUT MANAGEABLE CONSTRAINT ON PUBLIC FINANCES
The return of 10-year sovereign yields to levels not seen in 25 years —

nearing 5% in the United States, in the United Kingdom, and in France
— brings back to the forefront a constraint that had previously faded:
debt service, while the state of public finances is generally worse today
than it was a quarter-century ago, with budgets under heavy strain
and facing difficult trade-offs3. Among the trends to watch, the spread
between the implicit interest rate on debt and nominal GDP growth
— which has long been favorable (i.e. negative) — is set to reverse

by the end of the decade in most European economies. It would re -
main negative but narrow significantly in Spain and the United States,
with Japan standing out as the exception, still enjoying a significantly
favorable spread. Another trend to watch is the magnitude of the in -
crease in the interest burden by 2032, which is forecasted to double,
for example, in France, Germany, and Japan, and reach 5% of GDP in
the United States.
Nevertheless, this additional constraint remains manageable in the

short and medium term thanks to two factors:
i/ the protective inertia of the average debt maturity when it is high, as
is the case in the Eurozone, Japan, and the United Kingdom;
ii/ the onset of gradual fiscal consolidation in most European countries
(except in Germany, which is implementing an investment stimulus
program).
The projected trajectories are, however, demanding and subject to po-
litical uncertainties. But at least there is a stated commitment to redu-

cing fiscal imbalances. This is not the case in the United States, where,
in addition, the relatively short average maturity of the debt exposes
the country to a faster pass-through of rate hikes to debt service.
THREE INTERTWINED QUESTIONS SURROUNDING THE RISE IN LONG-TERM
RATES
The rise in long-term sovereign rates has multiple causes, some of
which are positive (resilient growth, the AI boom, risk appetite). Fur -
thermore, for now, it remains broadly warranted and orderly. And as

long as this rise occurs alongside a rally in equity markets, overall
financial conditions are not particularly tightened as a result.
Since the rise in long-term rates is driven by multiple factors, we
should expect them to remain, at a minimum, at a high level or even to
rise further, even if some of the factors that have recently pushed them
higher begin to subside. According to our forecasts, the bulk of the rise
is likely behind us, as yields already factor in the resilience of growth

and residual monetary tightening. Long-term rates could then begin to
decline as fiscal consolidation gains credibility.
The recent turbulence in the tech sector has, however, shown that
what is gained on one front (reduced pressure on long-term rates as
safe-haven bonds regain favor) immediately creates another problem
(increased downside risk to economic activity from a reversal of the
AI boom). What matters for growth is the overall trend in monetary

and financial conditions, including access to bank credit. Recent data
point to first signs of tighter conditions, but from a favorable starting
point, and this tightening is helpful to dampen demand and curb asso-
ciated inflation. As long as it remains contained — which is the most
likely scenario at present — it should only moderately dampen growth
momentum without jeopardizing it. In France, for example, we will
continue to monitor this interplay between tailwinds and headwinds

through the cross-dynamics of business start-ups and bankruptcies4.
Nonetheless, the bulk of these questions presents a new conundrum
for central banks: to what extent should they accommodate the rise
in long-term rates, and to what extent does this rise interfere with
the conduct of monetary policy? Positively, by improving transmission?
But then, to what extent can they rely on this rise to do part of the
job without fueling the rise excessively (by tightening too much or too

little)? Or negatively, by leading to an excessive tightening of financial
conditions and access to credit? All of this argues in favor of the very
measured approach adopted so far by central banks, particularly the
Fed and the ECB. Further hikes in policy rates are to be expected, but
rather a few ones than a lot, in order to fine-tune and support the cur-
rent growth cycle for as long as possible.
Hélène Baudchon
helene.baudchon@bnpparibas.com

《美國經濟數據表現強勁，支持美元短線維持高點》
美元指數在9月一波上漲過後呈現高檔盤整，10月
指數介於101.5-103內區間盤整。10月初美國公布新
增非農就業僅2.9萬人，疲弱的就業數據令利率市場
大幅降低10月升息預期。然而，美元並未因此而走
弱，10月匯市主軸環繞在歐元區政經風險。10/5西
班牙宣布提前於11/29改選，法國財政與政治危機、
中東局勢引發能源價格飆升，法國各區示威行動對經
濟成長帶來隱憂，法國10年期公債殖利率與德國公
市場投資展望

2
債殖利率利差創2011年歐債危機最嚴重時期以來最
高水準，10/22歐盟公布消費者信心指數再度下行，
政經不確定性因素令歐元短線承壓。
時序進入11月份，美國經濟數據表現穩定，耐久財
訂單創5個月以來最大單月增幅，加上消費信心連續
4個月走弱後首次回穩，10月商諮會消費者信心指
數大幅優於預期，分項數據顯示企業經營現況及展
望有所改善。然而，NielsenIQ數據顯示，萬聖節糖
果銷售較去年同期衰退-6.7%，油價及成本帶動商
品價格上升導致銷售疲弱。11 月可關注美國11/3期
中選舉過後消費動能是否回升。另一方面，美國製
造業PMI創53個月新高，但也同時顯示價格分項持

續上揚，商品與服務售價創2022年以來新高，短線
通膨壓力將支持利率維持在歷史相對高點一段時間，
預期美元年底前維持高檔震盪格局。

重點摘要
備註:ECB(歐洲央行)
• 法國政治局勢惡化
• 10月PMI對應歐元區GDP季增
率約0.3%
• 歐元年底目標價維持1.19
3
《歐元投資關鍵-法國政治風險令歐元走弱》
10月以來歐元區一連串的動盪導致歐元走弱。除了
西班牙提前大選，法國政治局勢惡化，極右派勝出
機率上升加學生示威遊行演變成各地零星暴動，經
濟下行風險升溫。法國政府提出目標將赤字占GDP
比從5.4%降至5%的方案，但反對黨已表態不支持。
目前執政黨在下議院非多數，財政政策停擺風險導
致公債殖利率大幅上升。法國10年期公債殖利率與
德國公債殖利率利差創2011年歐債危機最嚴重時期
以來最高水準。本行認為，法國右翼已放棄脫歐，
且強調財政紀律將遵守歐盟規範，法國選後議會仍

需籌組多數聯盟，財政惡化風險可控。
總經方面，歐元區經濟基本面持續轉佳。10月PMI
顯示經濟持續擴張，出口訂單連續第3個月增加，顯
示外需逐步改善。10月PMI對應歐元區GDP季增率
約0.3%，為近3年相對穩健的季度表現。通膨方面，
能源成本持續上升，企業為維持獲利而調升售價，
高利率逐漸抑制需求。德國央行最新通膨報告顯示，
目前複製2021-22年高通膨階段的可能性不大，但
通膨風險明顯偏向上行。展望後續，ECB 10月會議
維持利率不變，但通膨的僵固性將支持12月升息，
歐洲與美利差預期小幅收斂。基本面繼續支持ECB
升息的環境下，預計美歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐，
短線政治風險提供逢低布局時機，4Q26目標價1.19。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

重點摘要
• 維持英鎊中性評等，英鎊兌美
元年底目標1.34
4
《英鎊投資關鍵-英國經濟動能逐漸改善》
10月經濟數據方面，英國PMI初值顯示經濟動能持
續回升。綜合PMI升至53.1，創8個月新高。服務業
商業活動指數升至53.1，製造業產出指數升至53.1，
創25個月新高，製造業PMI升至52.8。新訂單成長
創2024年9月以來新高，帶動企業活動加速擴張，
並推動就業人數自2024年9月以來首度恢復成長。
然而，投入成本升至6月以來最高水準。主要受能源、
原物料與運輸成本上揚所帶動，且中東衝突與供應
鏈干擾延長交貨時間。企業對一年景氣展望樂觀，
但程度仍低於長期平均。經濟韌性增強及通膨壓力
居高不下，使英國央行將維持在貨幣緊縮的路徑，

且2027年進一步升息的可能性上升。對於英鎊的前
景逐漸樂觀，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

重點摘要

• 4Q26美元兌瑞郎目標價0.8
5
《瑞郎投資關鍵-瑞士景氣連續第7個月擴張》
瑞士9月製造業PMI下滑1.8點至55.3，但連續第7個
月維持在擴張區間。本次下滑主因產出、供應商交貨
時間及就業分項走弱。訂單積壓指數則持穩於57.0，
顯示需求仍具韌性，但企業持續去化庫存，原材料與
成品庫存皆低於50。採購價格指數升至70，反映電
子零組件及原物料價格上漲，中東地緣政治風險及AI
熱潮亦推升成本壓力。服務業PMI由58.3升至59.8，
維持強勁成長，新訂單與商業活動改善，就業指數升
至2025年4月以來高點。採購價格及銷售價格同步明
顯上升，顯示服務業通膨壓力有增溫跡象。整體而言，

瑞士通膨及成長前景溫和，央行並未因地緣政治風險
而強調通膨壓力，利差擴大為瑞郎走弱主因。央行對
升息態度保守，外需回溫與製造業改善正帶動瑞士經
濟展望持續好轉。4Q26美元兌瑞郎維持目標價0.8。

《日圓投資關鍵-股市波動升溫帶動避險情緒》
1個月以來全球科技股明顯回檔，美元兌日圓也從
160以上回到158，匯市在美國期中選舉前表現穩定，
日圓小幅偏強但呈區間整理格局。
日本央行在10月份的會議上維持政策不變。潛在通
膨和中長期通膨預期均已達到2%。日本央行也重申
金融環境依然寬鬆的觀點，這表明與聯準會和歐洲央
行相比，日本央行將更傾向於進一步升息。
10月22日那斯達克當日重挫-3%帶動日圓一度大幅
走強，但升幅未如7月及9月政府干預匯市之規模。
短線日圓投機部位已轉為淨多單，市場繼續預期日本
政府對於穩定匯市的決心。財務大臣片山表示，美元
兌日圓匯率較先前接近164時有所改善，當局將對無

序 或 過 度 投 機 行 為 採 取 行 動 。 本 行 認 為 ， 如果
USDJPY升至160以上，日圓買入干預的風險依然很

• 美元兌日圓4Q26目標價160
高。
政策方面，近期高市早苗政策面將從12月起，將汽
油補貼計畫下的基準汽油價格每月提高5日圓，但從
12月起恢復電力和天然氣補貼。整體而言，隨地緣
政治風險延續，政府控制汽油價格的措施將演變成一
場持久戰，政府將尋求透過動用儲備金和分階段縮減
汽油補貼來減輕財政負擔，但油價長期維持在高點將
繼續對財政帶來壓力。本行認為，日本財政政策及擴
大支出施政方針對日圓不利，惟美元兌日圓160為政
府可容忍的上緣，美元兌日圓4Q26目標價維持160。

• 自駕車、軟體營收增加帶動
車商獲利
美國傳統車商迎來皮卡改款週期，獲利表現有望提升
美國傳統車廠近年轉向生產更多高價卡車和SUV，因部分SUV和皮卡的調整後息稅前利潤率高達20%、超
過其他車型，也使美國新車平均交易價格在9月超過5萬美元。福特與通用汽車即將在2026年年底至2027
年迎來全尺寸皮卡改款與更新計畫，由於在推出新款或新一代卡車後，通常會有2~3年較佳的銷售期，預
估福特和通用汽車在2027年及之後的獲利有機會持續提升。
美國政策轉為支持燃油車發展，但高油價使油電、電動車需求提升

儘管美國政策轉向支持燃油車發展，但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下，使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔率
暴衝至16%，包括豐田、現代等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升。美國3Q26電動車銷量約佔新車總銷量
6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩腳步。由於油價上升使全球對電動車需求
增加，特斯拉2Q26、3Q26交車量也優於市場預期。
自駕車接受度提升，軟體營收增加帶動車商獲利
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接受度正持續提升，而各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱服務布局。通用
汽車預估其OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營收在2026年將突破30億美元，福特汽車的軟體付費訂閱用
戶已達160萬戶。3Q26特斯拉FSD活躍用戶數已達174萬、年增67%，且北美新車FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於10%的硬體銷售利潤率，軟體毛利率高達50~70%，且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。

美國9月份輕型車年化銷售量約為
1,600萬輛，低於市場預估共識的
1,630萬輛。儘管9月初的勞動節
促銷活動使購車回饋及零售銷售
增加，但同時銷售量年減4%也顯
示市場需求正面臨更大挑戰。
今年上半年儘管面臨高利率、高
車價等負面影響，但高收入族群
支撐使美國車市仍具韌性。然而
隨著利率上升、對消費者可負擔
性的懷疑日益增加，市場目前正
逐步將2026年美國輕型車銷售量
預估由1,610萬輛下修至1,560至
1,600萬輛區間。
聯準會在9月升息一碼，使美國
目標利率區間上升至3.75~4%，
且目前市場普遍預期聯準會在今
年12月及2027年上半年將再各升
息一碼。美國約有75~85%新車
購買使用貸款或融資，利率持續
上升將直接推高車貸利率，進而
增加消費者的購車成本與負債壓
力，且利率上升也會造成核貸門
檻轉趨嚴格，使消費者對新車的
需求下降。
未來若油價、通膨下降，市場重

新步入降息循環後有望使美國車
市需求重新回升。資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/9/24，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/22

3
福特、通用汽車全尺寸皮卡在改款當年及之後
兩年銷售表現優於同業
資料來源：Goldman Sachs，2026/10/13，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/22
車商將迎來皮卡改款週期
回饋優惠在交易價中佔比美國新車交易價格美國新車平均交易價格( ATP )在
9月超過5萬美元，車價上漲在每

年下半年為常見現象，以因應車
款更新而重新定價。然而，9月
ATP年增率低於1%遠低於典型的
9月增幅2~4%，顯示利率上升後
平價車款銷售相對強勁。
儘管美國新車售價成長幅度趨緩，
但其上升趨勢仍使美國傳統車商
利潤率逐步改善。通用、福特汽
車等傳統車廠近年轉向生產更多
卡車和SUV，因部分SUV和皮卡
的調整後息稅前利潤率超過20%，
使得車廠利潤率能維持一定水準。
(美元)
福特與通用汽車即將在2026年年
底至2027年迎來全尺寸皮卡改款
與 更 新 計 畫 ， 福 特 旗 艦 皮 卡 F-
150將換上全新3.0升V6引擎，通
用汽車則將對GMC Sierra 及
Chevrolet Silverado全尺寸皮卡
進行大改款。
鑑於福特與通用汽車在皮卡銷售
中取得的較高利潤，皮卡市場對
獲利的重要性可想而知。由於原
廠在推出新款或新一代卡車後，
通常會有2~3年較佳的銷售期，
預估福特和通用汽車在2027年及
之後的獲利有機會持續提升。

4
高油價使美國電動車銷售逐步回溫
資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28
需求使油電、電動車並存
康寧儘管政策轉向支持燃油車發展，
但自美伊戰爭後油價居高不下，
使油電混合車3Q26在美國的市佔
率暴衝至16%，包括豐田、現代
等品牌在美國的市占率持續上升。

而儘管美國3Q26電動車銷量較去
年同期下降(3Q25為電動車補助
期限，基期較高)，但仍較2Q26
成長近8%，約佔美國新車總銷量
的6.5%，高於2Q26的5.8%，顯
示電動車在美國市場正重新站穩
腳步。以市場反應來看，美國車
市正從先前單一電動化路線，轉
向燃油、油電、電動車並存局面。
美國國會在2025年9/30 正式取
消購買或租賃新電動車的7,500
美元稅收抵免，造成美國電動車
銷售在4Q25開始大幅下滑。另
外，川普政府在今年9月底正式
宣布放寬美國企業燃油效率標準，
將未來新車的燃油效率年均提升
目標下調，使部分車商開始降低
電動車生產以減少虧損。
傳統車企如福特、通用汽車的電
動車部門EBIT利潤率可能介於-
10~-30%以下，燃油效率標準放
寬使其生產方向轉回燃油車發展，
也有助於改善整體獲利結構。

通用汽車遞延收入上升，顯示未來軟體營收增加
資料來源：公司，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/29
軟體營收增加帶動獲利表現
各車商正積極擴大自駕與軟體訂閱
服務布局，並將其視為未來重要獲
利成長動能。 通 用 汽 車 預 估 其
OnStar和Super Cruise自駕輔助營

收在2026年將突破30億美元，並
在未來維持雙位數成長。福特汽車
的軟體付費訂閱用戶已達160萬戶。
3Q26特斯拉FSD活躍用戶數已達
174 萬 、 年增67% ， 且 北 美 新 車
FSD採用率超過75%。
相較傳統車商低於10%的硬體銷售
利潤率，軟體毛利率高達50~70%，
且訂閱制使車商部分營收轉為持續
性收入，將帶動其估值表現。
美國消費者對自動駕駛的認識與接
受度正持續提升。過去十年來，對
自駕車有較多了解的民眾比例由
40%增至58%，且近半數消費者相
信在有生之年將能看到自駕車普及。
另外，消費者雖然支持車輛自動化
發展，但仍傾向保有人工接管能力，
因此對Level 4 的 興 趣 最 高 ， 對
Level 5的興趣反而下降。
調查也發現，多數曾搭乘Robotaxi
的使用者對自動駕駛的觀感明顯改
善，未來隨著服務覆蓋範圍持續擴
大，Robotaxi有望進一步提升大眾
對自駕技術的信心與接受度。 資料來源：Cox Automotive，2026/9/28，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/29

谷歌廣告業務短期逆風壓抑指數

市場投資展望
《通訊服務龍頭短期營運雜音較多》
臉書 FY26Q3 財報營收、獲利皆優於預期。管理層
對 AI 投資變現的論述較上季更具體。AI 對廣告價
量的優化仍提供營收成長上行空間，但 Muse 規模
擴張與變現路徑短期變數仍多。谷歌同季財報反映
谷歌業務雙線推展的營運壓力。谷歌雲年增雖令人
驚豔，但搜尋業務營收出於預期的減速引發對做為
現金牛的搜尋業務競爭力的擔憂。隨著 AI Mode 相
關廣告業務全球推展、TPU 系統銷售爬坡與第三方
算力橋接陸續開始，谷歌短期雜音漸增。本行認為
兩龍頭短期上行動能較有限。但另一方面，本行認

為估值已反映大部分的逆風，與歷史估值區間對比
向下修正空間有限。因此，看法仍維持中立。
• 臉書 FY26Q3 財報優於預期，
Meta Muse 的投資變現較上
季更具體，但仍待相關廣告變
現實例與規模展開。
• 谷歌 FY26Q3 財報反映谷歌搜
尋、雲運算業務雙線推展的壓
力。市場將更加聚焦檢視雙線
的業務成長與獲利能力動態。
• 谷歌在傳統搜尋與 AI Mode /
AI Overview 搜尋廣告之間的
最佳化配置這關鍵議題上，將
迎來較多的不確定性。
1
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
通訊服務 中立 通訊服務主成分臉書財報優於預期且 Meta Muse的發展路徑更為具
體，廣告動能維持強健。然而，谷歌財報顯示在廣告、雲運算雙線發
展的壓力，同時搜尋廣告正迎來較多的不確定性。維持中立。
資料來源：Koyfin、Capital IQ，2026/09/29；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/29。
• 臉書 FY26Q3財報展現較具體的
Meta Muse 發展路徑 P.2

《臉書財報優於預期，谷歌財報較為錯綜…》
臉書 (META) FY26Q3 財報營收略優於預期、調整後
EPS 優於預期。但積極的資本支出擴張對獲利能力
的影響仍顯著。管理層指引顯示營收成長持續降溫。
自由現金流轉負，但資本支出低於市場預期。管理

層對 AI 投資變現的論述更聚焦在 Muse 為起點的規
模擴張帶動的商家交易與價值鏈環節滲透，較上季
更具體。在資本支出預期仍將持續，以在 FY2026-
27 最大化算力產能下，單位模型運算成本的下行曲
線將決定規模擴張的速度，值得持續留意模型迭代
與特化背後的成本下行發展，與商家接入臉書生態
系的速度。
谷歌 (GOOGL) 26Q3 財報反映谷歌業務雙線推展的
營運壓力。總營收雖約略符合共識預期，但營收組
成與預期有顯著差異。谷歌雲年增顯著高於預期，
但搜尋業務營收明顯減速且低於預期引發市場擔憂
搜尋業務競爭力。每股盈餘顯著低於預期，雖然 6-
7 成係因 SpaceX 持股的未實現評價損失，但因 AI
投資、TPU 系統銷售與搜尋成長減速導致利潤率也
大出市場預期之外。隨著 AI Mode 相關廣告業務全
球推展、TPU 系統銷售爬坡與第三方算力橋接陸續
開始，市場將更加聚焦檢視雙線的業務成長與獲利
能力動態。

反映在谷歌的搜尋廣告業務的成長上。此外，管理
層也提到 AI Mode / AI Overview 廣告的變現率仍

十一月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 PC零組件 ‧ 台灣

鴻海 
(2317.TW/2317 TT)

3Q26獲利率、EPS超前；AI依舊為 4Q26-2027年成長推手 
重要訊息 
鴻海 3Q26 每股盈餘 5.51 元，表現優於預期。有鑑於 AI 相關需求暢旺，公司看
好 4Q26 至 2027 年營運展望。 
評論及分析 
3Q26獲利創新高，超越市場預期。3Q26 EPS 為 5.51 元 (季增 29%、年增 
33%)，較凱基預估及市場共識高出 10-14%，主因利潤率穩健。毛利率 6% 
及營業利益率 3.8% 均優於凱基預估及市場共識，因營收增加、效率改善及
成本控制良好。3Q26 雲端暨網通營收占比升至 54%，營收年增 89%、季
增 28%，為營收成長主要動能；消費性電子 (CE) 及零組件營收分別占 
3Q26 營收的 29% 與 5%，亦是 3Q26 營收優於預期的因素，其中 CE 營收
年增 14%、季增 19%，零組件營收年增 20%、季增 10%。儘管投資收益
下滑及淨利息支出增加影響 3Q26 業外利益，3Q26 EPS 仍優於預期。1Q-
3Q26 EPS 為 13.35 元，年增 29%，營業利益率 3.7%，營業利益年增 
65%，優於指引。 
上修 2026 年營收財測；2027 年雲端伺服器展望依舊正向。管理層指引 
4Q26 營收將年增及季增逾 15%，其中 CE 營收季增最為強勁，因新 iPhone 
機種上市，而雲端暨網通及零組件營收將季增 5-14%，運算營收則將季
減。管理層亦指引 4Q26 AI 伺服器機櫃出貨量將季增逾  30%，出貨成長將
高於營收成長，因 ASP 低於 GB/VR AI 機櫃的 ASIC AI 機櫃成長是 AI 伺服
器的主要動能。管理層對 CE、運算及零組件 2026 年營收年增率指引皆上
修，主要反映零組件成本上升。2026-27 年營業利益率指引維持在 3%以
上，儘管新世代 Vera Rubin (VR) AI 伺服器機櫃可能稀釋毛利率。公司未提
供 2027 年的具體指引，但對雲端業務持正面看法，目標 AI 伺服器機櫃出
貨年增逾 30%，市占率較 2026 年的 40%以上再提升，而對其他業務則相
對審慎。凱基預期 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收占雲端伺服器營收將逾 60%，高
於 2026 年的 50% 以上，涵蓋 GPU 與 ASIC 機種，其中 ASIC 將佔 2027 
年 AI 伺服器營收的 20%以上或近總營收的 10%。鴻海客群亦將由 CSP 擴
大，新增部分 neocloud 客戶。加上垂直整合綜效，公司目標 2027 年營業
利益持續強勁年增，儘管利潤率比率呈下滑趨勢。為反映較強的營收，凱基
將 2026-27 年 EPS 預估分別調升至 19.54 元、23.89 元。 
投資建議 
凱基將鴻海目標價上調至 335 元，相當 2027 年每股盈餘的 14 倍。因公司
在 AI 伺服器市場的競爭力提升且 ROE 向上，我們維持「增加持股」評等。 
投資風險 
新一代 AI 產品進度延後；零組件供應受限；台幣升值。 
增加持股‧維持

焦點內容  
1. 鴻海 3Q26 每股盈餘 5.51 元，超前凱
基和市場預期，獲利率亦優於預期。 
2. 4Q26 營收成長指引為季增逾 15%，管
理層上修 2026 年 CE、運算及零組件
營收成長指引，以反映 ASP 上升。 
3. 雲端伺服器將帶動 2026-27 年營收、獲
利成長。我們預估 2027 年雲端業務的
營收佔比將由 2026 年的 52%擴大至
63%，動能來自 AI 伺服器新客戶與新
專案，以及 CPO 出貨增加。
